Every significant decision gets a row when it's made — this is the feedback loop that prevents drift.

| 105 | 2026-07-03 | Member-sheet QR (Damir question) — RECOMMENDATION: keep the QR OFF the default sheet. Flow: Accept → `setRequestAccepting(row)` latches the button to "Accepting…" (loading+disabled, aria-label synced) and disables Decline → `acceptContactRequest(listEl,state,req,opts)` removes the request and prepends a **handshaking chat** (excerpt `{type:'typing'}` "Establishing a quantum-secure handshake…", reusing the action-colored typing style + a gentle `[data-handshaking]` pulse, reduced-motion off) → the row is `aria-busy`, **un-openable** (tap routes to `onHandshakeBlocked`, never `onOpen`), **un-swipeable**, and shows only a **"Cancel handshake"** menu item → only the bridge signal `completeHandshake(...)` clears the flag, resets the excerpt, and bumps the timestamp so the just-secured contact surfaces on top. Timers live in the DEMO (mock bridge: 650ms ack → establishing → 2600ms complete), NOT the shell. **Recovery**: `failHandshake(...)` (symmetric to complete) removes a stranded chat — driven by the bridge failure/timeout signal (§9) OR the row's Cancel-handshake action — so a never-completing handshake is **never an un-removable trap** (the MAJOR the audit caught). Double-accept guarded (model `indexOf` + DOM disable); a late complete-after-cancel cannot resurrect a removed chat (triple guards). Desktop split-view: each pane (chat list · conversation · wallet · settings · apps) = its own native WebView; cross-pane coordination via C# (selectChat/selectTx), never a shared JS context; layout chrome (rail, dividers) may share a WebView but chat CONTENT never does. THE most important constraint in the project — supersedes any desktop "demo parity" ambition. |

HomePage: `onOverlayClosed` hook (Account close → setTheme/loadAvatar/shouldRefreshContacts; chat close → checkForRating); onChat unified (overlay tag "chat", wide col-1 / narrow full-span; dup-guard via getChatPage). | 234 | 2026-07-10 | 🟡 **RESUME/PRIVACY-LOCK BYPASS via the Cancel button (MAJOR — PRE-EXISTING, surfaced during the #230 lock scrutiny; NOT introduced by #227–#231; needs Damir on-device confirm + a design decision, per the #215 "verify on device" lesson).** Chain (all in the real tree): the RESUME lock is `new LockPage(true)` (App.xaml.cs:251) = `justConfirm` = **confirm mode**; `lock-shell.js lockSync` shows a **Cancel** button in confirm mode (`cancel.hidden = !confirm`, :123); `lock-page.js onCancel()` → `br.send('ixian:change')` (:55); `LockPage.onNavigating` for `ixian:change` under justConfirm fires `authSucceeded(this, false)` + closes the lock (`closeModalOverlay`/PopModal, :111-124); the resume lock's `authSucceeded` handler is `App.onUnlock`, which **ignores the bool** and unconditionally sets `isLockScreenActive=false` (App.xaml.cs:218-222). Net: tapping Cancel on the privacy lock dismisses it and unlocks the app WITHOUT the password — violates the ★ "lock must be un-dismissable" invariant. **Blast radius is limited to the resume lock:** the SettingsPage confirm-locks (delete wallet/account, lock-off) all check `e.Value`/`succeeded` and bail on false (`onDeleteWallet:299`, `onDeleteAccount:345`, `HandleAuthSucceeded:287`) — Cancel there safely aborts. Asymmetry proof: the BOOT lock is `new LockPage()` (unlock mode, no Cancel, only a "Use another wallet"→LaunchPage hatch) = correctly un-dismissable; only the resume lock's `true` opens the escape. **Why not a mechanical fix here:** `justConfirm` conflates two axes — (1) close-in-place-vs-rebuild-Home on success (resume wants close) and (2) show-a-Cancel-escape (resume must NOT). Guarding `App.onUnlock` on the bool alone is insufficient (Cancel still closes the lock → visible content with the latch stuck "locked"). The correct fix (resume lock in a confirm-close mode WITHOUT the Cancel escape, or a distinct lock mode) is a design/BE call. **✅ USER-CONFIRMED ON-DEVICE (Damir, 2026-07-10):** "when the app prompts lock + password you can bypass on Cancel; while actually REOPENING the app the lock screen doesn't have Cancel" — i.e. the resume/confirm lock's Cancel bypasses (confirmed) and the boot lock has no Cancel (confirmed un-dismissable). No longer needs confirmation; needs the fix decision. | Adversarial lock review (#233) + Damir on-device | 🟡 fix decision (BE, security-flagged #232); item 1 of `docs/fable-build-brief-desktop-pass.md` |
| 235 | 2026-07-10 | **BREAK-MY-VERDICT RE-AUDIT (Damir asked to spin the multi-agent loop over #233's two highest-risk CLEANs — the lock lifecycle + the linkify guard; 2 independent read-only agents, Read-tool-only). Both found REAL holes the single-pass #233 missed — 1 MAJOR fixed here, 3 flagged.** **(1) MAJOR FIXED (C#, landed) — in-place lock over a NON-HomePage host is back-dismissable.** `App.OnResume` stages the resume lock on `CurrentPage` (App.xaml.cs:257), which may be a pushed LEGACY page (Wallet Send/Scan/Backup/mini-app/…); `presentPreload`'s `canShowInPlace` allowed in-place whenever `NavigationStack.Last == op.host`, so the lock rendered inside the legacy page's Grid. But ONLY `HomePage.OnBackButtonPressed` swallows back while `hasModalOverlay()` (HomePage:1551) — every legacy page pops itself unconditionally (WalletSendPage:333, ScanPage:93, BackupPage:145, MiniAppPage:585, ContactDetails:213, +~15 more, verified) → hardware/gesture back pops the host page, tears down the lock stage, exposes content WITHOUT auth (+ fail-open aftermath: stale `modalOverlayOp` freezes HomePage back forever, `isLockScreenActive` stuck → all future resumes skip locking, leaked lock WebView). **Fix: `canShowInPlace` now also requires `op.host == overlayHost`** (SpixiContentPage.cs) — in-place only over the registered HomePage (back-guarded + cover-guarded); legacy-page hosts fall through to the real `PushModalAsync` (ModalStack sits above the page tree; the LockPage's own `OnBackButtonPressed` swallows back) — un-poppable. Settings-confirm locks unaffected (they host on HomePage via the overlay fallback, `op.host == overlayHost` holds). **(2) MAJOR FLAGGED (C#/BE, be-cutover C15) — confirm-modal link spoof via HtmlDecode.** `SingleChatPage.xaml.cs:344` runs `WebUtility.HtmlDecode(link)` AFTER the FE confirm modal already showed the pre-decode URL → `https://paypal.com&commat;evil.example.com/login` displays paypal-leading but `Browser.OpenAsync` navigates to host `evil.example.com` (userinfo trick via `&commat;`=@). Nullifies the #231c "confirm modal shows the true target" mitigation. Root fix = C# (don't HtmlDecode a URL, or decode BEFORE showing the user) + add an http/https scheme whitelist at the sink (NIT: `:337` only checks `.Contains("://")`, no scheme allowlist). Security-flagged → human BE review (#232). **(3) MINOR FLAGGED (FE) — `displayUrl` truncation hides the real host** (userinfo pushed into the elided middle: `google.com…@evil.com`); the confirm modal is the intended mitigation (weakened by #2). **(4) MINOR FLAGGED (FE) — quadratic `matchAll` scan** on a crafted ~50KB "a.a.a…" no-TLD token → chat-pane render freeze (isolated to the chat WebView per §1); add a length guard before linkify. Both agents' XSS/DOM surface + regex-exponential-ReDoS + mention/link interplay = CONFIRMED SAFE (DOM-built, textContent only, no innerHTML; scheme injection into the FE href impossible — all URL_RE alts yield http(s)://).
